It just... it kind of spills over into everything, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control your worrying when you want to?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I try to... I try to tell myself to just stop, you know? Just focus on one thing. But... it doesn't work. One worry just kind of... leads right into another one. And then... I end up just checking my calendar, and my budget, and my work assignments... over and over. Instead of actually getting anything done. So... no, I can't really control it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or fearful when you're worrying about these everyday things?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose so. I don't know if I'd use the word fearful, but... yeah. My mind just keeps jumping around. Like... I'll be worrying that I'm falling behind at work, and then it just switches to... whether I'm wasting my money on school. And then I start thinking about my family, and if they're going to think I can't handle things. It's just... I end up treating every little issue like it's going to completely derail my whole future. So... I guess that makes me anxious, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worrying has been present for more than six months?\n\nPatient: Yeah... it has. Um... probably for about a year and three months now. That's... that's when I started my evening graduate certificate program. I kept my full-time job as an analyst, so... yeah. It's been going on since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems at work or in your personal relationships because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. I mean... I've been missing some deadlines at work lately. And... falling behind in my classes. I just... I don't really do the things I used to do anymore. Like yoga, or running... I don't even really cook now. I just... don't."
